# Registry artifact verification

This report is generated by `scripts/verify-registry-artifacts.py`. It re-derives, from the bytes each registry URL actually serves, whether `install_type`, `bundle_path` and `download_type` describe the artifact they point at.

- Generated: `2026-09-26T02:12:21+00:00` (tool v1.1.0)
- Registry root: `/Users/andreanjos/apm/registry/plugins`
- Limits: download cap `67108864` bytes, request timeout `25.0s`, concurrency `4`, 3 attempts per request, host spacing `0s`, spacing after a `429` `3.0s`
- Nothing is installed, no DMG is mounted, no privileged command is run.
- Merged from `data/registry-verification-direct-wave2.json` (`direct`, 372 entries, generated `2026-09-25T22:37:01+00:00`): cap `67108864` bytes, timeout `25.0s`, concurrency `6`, 3 attempts, host spacing `0s`, spacing after a `429` `0s`
- Merged from `data/registry-verification-manual.json` (`manual`, 13503 entries, generated `2026-09-26T02:01:40+00:00`): cap `268435456` bytes, timeout `15.0s`, concurrency `8`, 2 attempts, host spacing `0s`, spacing after a `429` `3.0s`
- Merged from `data/registry-verification-managed.json` (`managed`, 2412 entries, generated `2026-09-26T00:16:36+00:00`): cap `67108864` bytes, timeout `20.0s`, concurrency `8`, 3 attempts, host spacing `2.0s`, spacing after a `429` `0s`
- This run proposes 0 field corrections, all on `download_type = "direct"` records — the only scope `--apply` rewrites, because a `direct` record claims apm can fetch the URL itself. A run after `--apply` should propose none for the fields it already fixed.
- It also lists 22 `manual` records whose URL answers with an artifact; 22 are promotion candidates. None were rewritten.

## Method and limits

Each format entry is probed over HTTP with redirects followed (status, content-type, content-length, `Content-Disposition`); the first bytes identify the container from its magic, and ZIPs are listed either from a full download (under the cap) or from the archive's central directory in the tail (over the cap). PKGs are decoded from their xar TOC and cpio payload, so the bundle names a package installs are known without installing it. DMGs are never mounted, so their contents stay unverified.

- Re-runnable and idempotent: downloaded bytes are cached under `data/registry-verification-cache/`, results are sorted, and no state outside the repo changes.
- No plugin is installed, no bytes are written under `~/.apm` or the apm data dir, no `sudo`.
- Bounded: 67108864 byte download cap per artifact, 25.0s per request, 4 concurrent workers.
- `--apply` is the only mode that edits the registry, and only from recorded evidence.
- Rate limits and transient server errors (408/425/429/5xx) are retried up to three times, honouring `Retry-After` when the host sends one; a `HEAD` that a host rejects is confirmed with a ranged `GET`.
- A host that answers `429` is spaced out from then on (`--pace-on-429`, default 3s): a rate limiter that rejects every request in a burst cannot be retried out of, so the burst is what has to change. `--host-interval` applies the same spacing to every host.
- One probe is made per distinct URL, and every format entry pointing at that URL reuses it: a plugin's formats and releases share one artifact, and a vendor page is often the download page for a whole catalogue.

### Container policy for `install_type`

`install_type` says how apm handles the downloaded archive, so a nested container is only re-typed when no apm path can consume the wrapper:

| Artifact | `install_type` | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ZIP of plugin bundles | `zip` | core extracts and places the bundle |
| ZIP wrapping a PKG | `zip` | `apm-core`'s ZIP path finds the nested `.pkg` and routes it to the privileged pkg handler, so `apm install` prompts for admin; re-typing it as `pkg` would hand a `.zip` to `installer -pkg` and break installs that work today |
| ZIP wrapping a DMG | `dmg` | no apm path unwraps a nested DMG, so the artifact's real installer type is a DMG (it remains non-installable by apm either way) |
| Flat PKG (xar) | `pkg` | `installer -pkg`, privileged |
| DMG | `dmg` | mounted and copied |
| URL serving HTML/404 on a `direct` record | type unchanged; `download_type` becomes `manual` | nothing to install |
| URL serving HTML on a `managed` record | unchanged | `managed` means the vendor manager app installs it, which the record's `installer` declares |

### Why the `managed` wave is reported but never retyped

`download_type = "managed"` does not claim the URL serves an artifact; it claims the plugin is installed through a vendor manager app. Every managed record here is backed by an `installer` key in `registry/installers.toml` that carries that app's `/Applications` paths, and the URL is the vendor's own page, so an HTML answer confirms the record instead of refuting it. Retyping one to `manual` would drop the vendor-manager handoff that makes the record installable:

- managed format entries: 2412 across 1206 plugins
- entries whose plugin declares an installer key present in `installers.toml` with non-empty `app_paths`: 2412 of 2412
- entries whose URL host is the vendor's own host (as declared by the installer's `homepage`): 2410 of 2412
- entries whose URL is exactly the page `installers.toml` lists as the installer's `download_url`: 398; the rest are that vendor's product or support pages for the individual plugin
- installer keys in play: `ua-connect` (666), `waves-central` (552), `softube-central` (280), `native-access` (196), `spitfire-audio` (196), `arturia-software-center` (158), `plugin-alliance-installation-manager` (130), `izotope-product-portal` (98), `roland-cloud` (82), `toontrack-product-manager` (54)

A managed URL that 404s is a broken pointer in a vendor page, not proof that the plugin has no artifact, so it is reported under per-entry status and left alone.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `download_type = "direct"` | 372 | 190 | 29 | every unique URL downloaded and inspected under the cap |
| 2 | `download_type = "managed"` | 2412 | 849 | 11 | every unique URL probed; artifacts listed/inspected under the cap. No rewrite: the URL is the vendor page the manager-app flow opens, not the artifact apm installs |
| 3 | `download_type = "manual"` | 13503 | 4556 | 523 | every unique URL probed; the ones that answer with an artifact are listed as promotion candidates (no rewrites) |

44 of 13503 `manual` entries already point at a fetchable artifact (an archive or installer rather than a product page). They are listed under per-entry status as promotion candidates; none of them were rewritten.

## Promotion candidates in the `manual` wave

`download_type = "manual"` says the user fetches the plugin by hand; a record whose URL answers with the installer itself is mislabelled, and the returned bytes say so. These are proposals: the URL of a `manual` record is where apm sends the user, so retyping it changes the install path and belongs to the maintainer. Nothing here was applied. Candidates are probed with the same limits as the rest of the run, so a blocker that names the download cap or the DMG rule needs a different pass, not a different judgment.

## Checksum state

- `match`: 214 entries whose downloaded bytes hash to the declared `sha256`
- `mismatch`: 16 entries whose declared `sha256` is wrong
- `not-computed`: 16049 entries where no bytes were hashed

### Entries whose declared checksum no longer matches the served bytes

`apm` verifies the digest after downloading and deletes the archive on a mismatch (`ApmError::Checksum`, `crates/apm-core/src/engine/install_download.rs`), so these records cannot install today. The served bytes are real; the declared digest is for an older build of the same URL.

| `https://fullbucket.de/music/dl.php?file=qyooo_1_2_1_mac` | 2 | `e0fcde90d6516d6c…` | `c32e74220ba4a472…` | 4601133 | `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl/qyooo_1_2_2_mac.pkg` |
| `https://tal-software.com/downloads/plugins/TAL-BassLine-101-installer_m…` | 2 | `d3dba9e92a8c0c5a…` | `1bca65f194b2372e…` | 27911735 | `https://tal-software.com/downloads/plugins/TAL-BassLine-101-installer_m…` (no redirect) |

| `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl.php?file=fb3100_1_2_1_mac` | 2 | `0eb7b8d3ff5febd4…` | `548352e42fa6ed14…` | 14309145 | `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl/fb3100_1_2_2_mac.pkg` |
| `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl.php?file=fbdelay_2_0_3_mac` | 2 | `0cddf534ef094aa0…` | `9b7829cda9a98216…` | 5515488 | `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl/fbdelay_2_0_3_mac.pkg` |
| `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl.php?file=whispair_1_1_5_mac` | 2 | `79e710986d22c78b…` | `23e39f384fe55898…` | 15061971 | `https://www.fullbucket.de/music/dl/whispair_1_1_5_mac.pkg` |

**Policy options (proposed — nothing below was applied)**

1. **Leave them.** `apm install` keeps refusing on mismatch. Honest about the bytes, but it reports a vendor re-release as possible corruption/tampering, retries forever, and leaves the plugin permanently uninstallable.
2. **Re-verify and update `sha256`.** Installs work again, but it blesses whatever the vendor served at that moment with no review; where the resolved URL names a different build than the record does (see the rows above that show a resolved URL), it re-versions the record in place while its `version` field still names the old build. The same rot returns on the vendor's next in-place roll.
3. **Mark the entry stale.** Stop declaring a digest apm cannot keep: set `sha256 = "manual"` (the placeholder vocabulary `is_placeholder_sha256` already recognises, so no schema change) and `download_type = "manual"` for the affected formats, keeping the URL. `apm install` then hands the user the vendor's download instead of promising a verified one, and no byte-following maintenance is needed. It loses automatic install for records that fail today anyway.

Recommendation: **option 3**, split by cause. The URLs whose redirect resolves to a differently-named newer build are a *version roll*, and the right fix there is a new release with the resolved URL and its digest — that is curation, and it is how such a record legitimately returns to `direct`. The URLs that serve a same-named file with different bytes are a re-packaged artifact with no honest digest to pin, so they should be marked stale (`download_type = "manual"`, `sha256 = "manual"`). A blanket checksum refresh and leaving them alone are both worse: the first silently re-versions and re-rots, the second leaves a permanently broken install that the user cannot distinguish from a working one.

## Registry source precedence (second fix)

The configured sources disagree per slug. On this machine the curated registry (`registry/plugins/**`, source `official`) and a scraped catalogue (`data/registry`, source `scraped`) both define most slugs, and the scraped record always wins when it is loaded last:

- scraped records: 7988 (7988 declare `license = "commercial"`, 7988 declare `is_paid = true`). That is the scraper's template default (`scripts/generate-registry.py`, `scripts/enrich-registry.py`), not evidence about the plugin, and the scraped records also ship empty download URLs. The scraped catalogue and the scrapers live in gitignored paths (`data/`, `scripts/`), so the durable fix belongs in the merge rule rather than in that data.
- slugs defined by both sources: 7588 of 8090 curated slugs
- curated machine-fetchable format entries shadowed by a scraped record: 996 — those installs resolve to an empty URL and `download_type = "manual"`

Precedence is now explicit and deterministic: sources are ordered by priority (built-in `official` first, then user sources in `config.toml` order) and the **highest-priority source that defines a slug owns the merged record**. Lower-priority sources only add slugs the official registry does not define; their records stay readable through `Registry::find_in_source` / `plugins_by_source`. To make a fork authoritative, point `default_registry_url` at it. This is pinned by `lower_priority_source_cannot_reclassify_a_curated_free_plugin` and `load_all_sources_tracks_source_specific_provenance` in `crates/apm-core/src/registry/mod.rs`.

## Observed but not applied

A probe of the URL cannot retype a `managed` or `manual` record: a `managed` record is installed by the vendor's manager app through its `installer` entry (see above), and a `manual` URL is where apm sends the user rather than what apm installs. These mismatches are therefore reported and deliberately left unchanged (2 fields).

| Entry | Scope | Field | Declared | Observed | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `vcv-rack:au` | `manual` | `formats.au` | `present` | `absent` | artifact (download) contains no .component bundle but does contain ._VCV Rack 2 Free.app, VCV Rack 2 Free.app |
| `vcv-rack:vst3` | `manual` | `formats.vst3` | `present` | `absent` | artifact (download) contains no .vst3 bundle but does contain ._VCV Rack 2 Free.app, VCV Rack 2 Free.app |

### Format claims that need a new format block

For these records every declared format is absent from the artifact, so removing them would leave the record with no formats at all. The artifact proves the declared formats are wrong; the fix is to declare the format the artifact actually carries (for example `[formats.app]` for an installer-app package), which is a schema change rather than a field correction, so it is reported instead of applied.

| Entry | Field | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `dpf-plugins@1.3:au` | `formats.au` | artifact (download) contains no .component bundle but does contain DPF-Plugins-v1.3-macOS/3BandEQ.vst, DPF-Plugins-v1.3-macOS/3BandSplitter.vst, DPF-Plugins-v1.3-macOS/AmplitudeImposer.vst, DPF-Plugins-v1.3-macOS/CycleShifter.vst, DPF-Plugins-v1.3-macOS/Kars.vst, DPF-Plugins-v1.3-macOS/MVerb.vst |
